    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:methodology}\n\n\\subsection{Base Architecture}\n\\label{sec:base_arch}\n\nAll experiments build on DreamerV3 \\citep{hafner2023dreamerv3}, which learns a recurrent state-space model (RSSM) with discrete latent states ($32$ categories $\\times$ $32$ classes), uses symlog-transformed predictions, and trains an actor-critic entirely within imagined trajectories. The standard configuration imagines $H{=}15$ steps at a fixed temporal resolution of $\\dt{=}1$, meaning each imagination step corresponds to one environment transition.\n\n\\subsection{Variable-Rate Imagination Mechanism}\n\\label{sec:variable_rate}\n\nWe introduce three orthogonal architectural modifications that enable variable-rate imagination in DreamerV3. Each can be independently toggled to isolate its contribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal embedding.}\nA learned embedding $e(\\dt)$ is concatenated to the RSSM input at each imagination step to condition state predictions on the temporal scale. We test three modes:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Trained}: embedding parameters are updated via backpropagation through the world model loss, so the RSSM can co-adapt its dynamics and reward predictions to the temporal scale.\n\\item \\textbf{Frozen}: a fixed random projection of identical dimensionality (\\texttt{requires\\_grad=False}). The RSSM receives a temporal signal but cannot co-adapt to it.\n\\item \\textbf{Removed}: embedding dimension set to zero; no temporal conditioning signal is provided.\n\\end{itemize}\nWe further distinguish \\textbf{trained-and-exposed} (subsample ON: the RSSM trains on $\\dt{>}1$ transitions) from \\textbf{trained-but-unexposed} (subsample OFF: $\\dt{>}1$ used only during imagination). This four-way classification (trained/exposed, trained/unexposed, frozen, removed) is the key experimental structure of our factorial design.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal subsampling.}\nWhen enabled, the replay buffer augments training data with variable-$\\dt$ transitions by subsampling consecutive frames. Concretely, with a maximum subsampling factor of~2, the RSSM trains on both $\\dt{=}1$ and $\\dt{=}2$ transitions drawn from the same episodes. When disabled, only standard $\\dt{=}1$ transitions are used. This factor controls whether the world model has seen multi-scale dynamics during training.\n\n\\paragraph{Imagination horizon and step size.}\nThe imagination horizon $H$ sets the number of rollout steps. We test $H{=}15$ (default) and $H{=}8$. The reduced horizon controls for compounding error: $H{=}8$ steps at $\\dt{=}2$ covers $16$ physical time steps, comparable to the $15$ physical steps of the default configuration. The parameter \\texttt{gate\\_fixed\\_dt} forces all imagination steps to use a specific $\\dt$ value ($1$, $2$, or $4$); this separates the effect of $\\dt$-conditioning from any learned gating policy.\n\n\\subsection{Factorial Design}\n\\label{sec:factorial}\n\nWe adopt a factorial ablation across four factors: $\\dt$-embedding mode (trained / frozen / none), temporal subsampling (on / off), imagination horizon ($H{=}15$ / $H{=}8$), and imagination step size ($\\dt{=}1$, $2$, $4$). The design (12 conditions, Table~\\ref{tab:factorial_design} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial}) isolates trained $\\dt$-embedding and multi-rate data exposure as orthogonal factors. Key configurations use 4 seeds; following \\citet{agarwal2021precipice}, we report per-seed trajectories.\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Metrics}\n\\label{sec:metrics}\n\n\\paragraph{Normalized value-to-eval ratio ($\\nve$).}\nWe define $\\nve_t = (v/e)_{\\text{config},t} / (v/e)_{\\text{baseline},t}$, where $v/e$ is the critic's mean imagined value divided by mean evaluation return, and the baseline is the highest-performing seed (exp\\_001, seed~1, 505K steps). We restrict to a \\textbf{35K+ window} to avoid early-training inflation. The $\\nve \\geq 1.5\\times$ threshold achieves 27/30 accuracy (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, mean AUROC${=}0.981$, Appendix~Table~\\ref{tab:nve_loeo}).\n% Note: AUROC computed on original n=29 validation set; exp_059 (s5) correctly classified under same threshold\n%% TODO: Recalculate LOEO mean AUROC with n=30 (exp_059 added as CIVO-positive)\n\\subsection{Selective Gradient Stopping}\n\\label{sec:sgs_method}\n\nTo identify which gradient pathway through $e_{\\Delta t}$ drives CIVO, we introduce \\textit{selective gradient stopping} (SGS): $e_{\\Delta t}$ is detached via $\\operatorname{sg}(\\cdot)$ before selected prediction heads during the backward pass, preventing those loss gradients from updating $e_{\\Delta t}$ while preserving its forward-pass contribution. We test three variants: reward-only (exp\\_016e), full-pathway (exp\\_018, all non-KL gradients blocked), and obs-decoder-only (exp\\_025/026). Gradient decomposition using $\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}_i$ quantifies each pathway's contribution (Appendix~\\ref{sec:sgs_gradient_analysis}).\n\n\\subsection{Forward-Pass Interventions}\n\\label{sec:forward_pass_method}\n\nWe test two forward-pass interventions as representation- and output-level probes: \\textbf{TCN} adds Gaussian noise $\\mathcal{N}(0, \\sigma^2)$ to $e_{\\Delta t}$ during imagination ($\\sigma \\in \\{0.5, 0.2\\}$, three environments); \\textbf{RAVE} clips imagined values exceeding an EMA anchor ($c \\in \\{2.0, 1.5\\}$).\n\n\\subsection{Dual-Critic Mechanism Ablation}\n\\label{sec:dual_critic_method}\n\nTo test whether standard overestimation countermeasures address CIVO, we conduct a $2{\\times}2$ factorial: \\{single, dual\\} $\\times$ \\{narrow (${\\sim}1.2$M), wide (${\\sim}2.4$M)\\} critic parameters. The dual-critic variant follows TD3 \\citep{fujimoto2018td3}: two independent value heads trained on shared RSSM features, with the actor optimizing $\\min(v_1, v_2)$. The wide variant doubles hidden dimensions to control for capacity independently of the min operation.\n\n\\subsection{Environment and Training Details}\n\\label{sec:env_details}\n\nAll experiments use Walker Walk and Cheetah Run from the DeepMind Control Suite (DMC; \\citealt{tassa2018dmcontrol}) with proprioceptive observations, chosen for their contrasting dynamics (6-joint biped vs.\\ 2-joint runner). Walker Walk serves as the primary environment with the full factorial design; Cheetah Run serves as a severity-dependent generalization probe: CIVO manifests at $8$--$70\\times$ NVE on Cheetah versus $2$--$5\\times$ on Walker, testing whether interventions transfer under amplified severity rather than replicating the full factorial. The baseline runs for 500K steps (peak return 970.4); treatments run for 105K steps, sufficient to observe collapse onset. All hyperparameters follow the DreamerV3 defaults \\citep{hafner2023dreamerv3} without task-specific tuning ($d{=}32$ temporal embedding; full hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{sec:hyperparameters}). Training uses a single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPU (${\\sim}80$ GPU-hours total). The codebase extends the official DreamerV3 implementation\\footnote{\\url{https://github.com/danijar/dreamerv3}, accessed March 2026.} with the variable-rate modules described in \\S\\ref{sec:variable_rate}.\n",

We restrict to a \\textbf{35K+ window} to avoid early-training inflation. The $\\nve \\geq 1.5\\times$ threshold achieves 27/30 accuracy (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, mean AUROC${=}0.981$, Appendix~Table~\\ref{tab:nve_loeo}).",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe identify trained $\\dt$-embedding with multi-rate data exposure as the strongest predictor of CIVO in DreamerV3 (27/30, AUROC${=}0.953$; LOEO cross-validated). No tested intervention reliably eliminates CIVO; in pure imagination, dual critics converge ($\\rho > 0.999$), rendering Double Q pessimism ineffective. The feedback architecture---MPC re-planning versus imagination-based bootstrapping---is the hypothesized structural factor. Empirical validation on MPC-based architectures (e.g., TD-MPC2) remains future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nOur analysis covers proprioceptive locomotion (DMC) and simulated manipulation (ManiSkill) but does not test pixel-based observations, Atari benchmarks, or real-robot deployments where sensor noise and actuation latency introduce additional confounds.\nAll experiments use DreamerV3's RSSM; whether CIVO manifests in other imagination-based architectures (e.g., IRIS \\citep{micheli2023iris} or latent-space planners beyond TD-MPC2) remains open.\nWe characterize CIVO under fixed-$\\dt$ subsampling; learned temporal gates warrant separate study.\nStatistical power with 4 seeds per configuration is limited \\citep{colas2018seeds}; the NVE threshold (LOEO AUROC${=}0.981$) may require recalibration for different reward scales.\n%% TODO: Recalculate LOEO mean AUROC with n=30 (exp_059 added as CIVO-positive)\nCode: upon acceptance.\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n",

docs/paper/sections/methodology.tex:38:We define $\nve_t = (v/e)_{\text{config},t} / (v/e)_{\text{baseline},t}$, where $v/e$ is the critic's mean imagined value divided by mean evaluation return, and the baseline is the highest-performing seed (exp\_001, seed~1, 505K steps). We restrict to a \textbf{35K+ window} to avoid early-training inflation. The $\nve \geq 1.5\times$ threshold achieves 27/30 accuracy (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, mean AUROC${=}0.981$, Appendix~Table~\ref{tab:nve_loeo}).
docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:252:To assess sensitivity to baseline seed choice, we extend the single-seed baseline (seed~1, eval 915.7 at 105K) to three additional seeds trained to 505K steps (Table~\ref{tab:baseline_seeds}).
docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:256:\caption{Walker Walk baseline seeds at 105K and 505K steps.}
docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:261:\textbf{Seed} & \textbf{Eval@105K} & \textbf{Eval@505K} & \textbf{ve@65K} & \textbf{ve@105K} \\
docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:275:\paragraph{Normalization conventions.} Walker NVE is normalized against the single-seed baseline (seed~1, exp\_001, 505K steps). Cheetah NVE is normalized against a 5-seed mean baseline (seeds~0--4, each run to ${\sim}$210K steps). The differing seed counts reflect available baseline data; both provide sufficient coverage of the respective experimental windows (Walker: 210K analysis window; Cheetah: 105K analysis window).
docs/paper/sections/results.tex:4:We organize results around seven findings (F1--F7) that establish a causal chain from trained temporal conditioning to pathological value dynamics. All experiments use Walker Walk (DMC) with DreamerV3; treatments run to 105K steps and the baseline to 505K steps. We report evaluation returns (mean over 10 episodes) and the normalized value-to-eval ratio ($\nve$, \S\ref{sec:metrics}).

_source line 103 · 2026-05-24T08:34:28.835Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019hTCDyNtFgYrRwxnYfhDyq`</summary>

```text
0	World-model reinforcement learning is increasingly deployed for robot control, where temporal abstraction enables planning across heterogeneous time scales. We find that $\Delta t$-conditioning, effective in model-predictive control (MPC), cannot transfer to imagination-based actor-critic training: every variable-rate DreamerV3 configuration degrades to 14--19\% of baseline. Factorial diagnostics on two continuous-control tasks identify trained temporal embedding with multi-rate data exposure as the strongest predictor of this failure---\emph{conditioning-induced value overestimation} (CIVO; 27/30, AUROC${=}0.953$, LOEO cross-validated). The RSSM develops a reward prediction gap at $\Delta t{>}1$ that the critic internalizes as optimistic values; the contrast with MPC identifies feedback architecture as a hypothesized structural factor. No tested intervention across four levels (gradient, output, representation, architecture) reliably eliminates CIVO: dual critics converge in pure imagination, rendering Double Q pessimism ineffective, though min $+$ increased capacity enables recovery.
1	
```

</details>

3	
4	We identify trained $\dt$-embedding with multi-rate data exposure as the strongest predictor of CIVO in DreamerV3 (27/30, AUROC${=}0.953$; LOEO cross-validated). No tested intervention reliably eliminates CIVO; in pure imagination, dual critics converge ($\rho > 0.999$), rendering Double Q pessimism ineffective. The feedback architecture---MPC re-planning versus imagination-based bootstrapping---is the hypothesized structural factor.
